                   Mr . Harold O' BRIAN · Reported U.P.O. Sightin~
                   Near Prince George, B. C. 78·11·18
                   PRINCE GBORGB DETACHMENT CASE (UNORG.)

            1.       On 18 November, 1978, at 2030 hours,. Mr. O'BRIAN contacted
            this offic~, adv1sing that there was a U.P.O. over his property.
            Mr. O' BRIAN's property is approximately 20 rniles Bast of Prince
            George, B. C..
            2.      At 2110 hours,' Cst. K. G. KBIDEMA arrived at O' Brian 's
            residence , and Mr. and Mrs. O' BRLAN gave the following account of
            the sighting:
                     The entire family was in the basembnt, playing darts.
            Mrs. O' BRIAN came upstairs at approximately 2010 hours ·to get
            something to eat,and noticed a bright light to the east. Mrs.
            O'BRIAN cal l ed the family upstairs , and everyone watched it from
            the large living·rooa window .
                     They describe the light at fir s t as a bright red, and then
            it appeared to ,ove closer, and the colour appeared to be a fire
•           orange·red. The lights were extremely bright, almost like the light
            cast from an arc welder. The field where the UPO appeared to be
            hover ing is cl~ared, and approximately 8 acres in size.
                  O'BRIAN stated that the lights lit up the entire field al·
        ' most as though it were daylight. The U.P.O. ~ppeared to be ap-
          proxiaately 85 ' ~o'l00 ' above the ground, and reaained above the
          field for what O'BRIAN feels was approximately 15 ·20 mindte s~

                               Mr. O'BRIAN and Cst. HEIDEMA conducted a search of the field
                       where the U.P.O. appeared to have· been, however, nothing at all was
                       disturbed. There was no indication of any snow being blown by any
                       tu~bulance, no snow knocked from the top of trees , etc.

·.                               O'BRIAN could no~ give an ac ~ urate esti mate of the size
     \                 of the U.P.O., due to the fact ~hat 1t was hard to establi~h it'~
                       distance from the house , however, he feels tt was approfimately
                       100 .t t. in diameter.           ·         I•           .
                       3.       The weather sonditions at the time of the sigh ting were:
                                Clear visibility; Cold, -30°C; 3/4 moon (very bright);
                                no wind; fresh snow on ground (approximately eight to
                                ten inches) .

         .,            U.P . O. He appeared to be a rational person, and it is doubtful
                       that he or his family would fa &ricate their r eport.

                                               78 DEC 20
                  'At 0100 hours this date, ·Mr. Gilbert HEFFERN of 14 Grieve
      Gander, Nfld ., contacted Gander Airport Detachment and advised that he
      his girlfriend, JudyiMarie HISCOCK of 65 Hawker Cres., GanderL Nfld. L
      seen an Unidentified Flying Object in the sky in the area of ~oulis Hro
      on the Trans Canaqa Highway approximately twelve miles east of Gander.
      The object was first noticed by Mr. HEFFERN approximately one half mile
      South of the rrrans, Canada llighway. It appeared to travel over their auto
      and hoover for a shprt· period of time and wa~ lost from sight . Mr.
      advised that when he first noticed the object he tnought it was an airpl
      but after looking at' it Iurther he changed ~s mind. Object was observed
      about one and one half minutes from a moving vehicle . He advised that
      and his girlfriond pannicked shortly after noticing object and he inc

      Roclty WRITB -
      Sighting of UPO
      Labrador City, Nfld. 1 -       7~    02 24


                At approximately l0a40 p.m. on 24 th of ll'ebruary, 1979 l!!llT Mr. Rooky
      WlllT£ of 4010 Duley cres., Labrador City ;-iifid. reported seeJ.ng an UPO in this
      area. At the time of the sighting the sky wae very c :t,,ar.

the house looking throuah the kitchen window. The window in the kitchen
goes South West. I ~aw a light slishtly larger than the stars; slightly '

       On the 19 day of July , 1979 at approximately 23:15 I was getting
ready for bed'when Nick called me from t he kitchen. He asked me to come
at somet-1ng . I looked out the kitchen window with him and I ,saw it . To
me, it looked like a large star , just about ' the tree line, It was moving .
lt was npt mov1ng all the tim,e , just- periodically . It would dash away
t he n come back to where about it started going up and down , side ways .
It seemed so far. away that 1n order to move the way it did, it would have
to be going a~ a tremendous speed . I went outdoors with the binoculars
it see. . that i could see redish light but without the binocular I oould
not see any radish lights . I observed it for just about 20 minutes and
then we turned away then turned back and it was gone .

                                     Re:    Possible UFO Sighting         '

                              On September 01, 1979 Cornelius BLANCHARD
                of Cold Brook, Newfo undland, DOD: 39 APR 0 3 , c alled this
                office advising that last night aro und OOhJO he observed
                a strange ~henomenon. •                         se/(J 1 , Q'( 3D
                2.             Mr.s . Rita BLANCHARD stated that s he tho ught
                she heard a noise' o utside her window. The noise s o unded
                like a screec h. When she loplted through the window there
                was a reddish light in the c louds.
   "            3.            The lighted object dropped and in about Uve
                minute• it appeared to be down to the top of the trees • The
                object was about 500 feet from th~ house. The light would
                disappear and shortly afterward, would reappear in the same
                a r ea. ~h was observed by both Mr : and Mrs. BLANCBAR.D.
                4.            Mrs. BLANCHARD stated the ob ject appeared to
                be about 20 feet wide while Mr . BLANCHARD stated that the
                object was about 3 to 4 feet wide. Be stated the object was
                like looking into the sun. 'l'he colour of the object was that
                of the sun setting in the evening.
                5.             BLANCHARD searched th-e ~ea the next day but
                did not- notice anything out of the ordin~ry.
                6.            A check with the weather station revealed there
                was light rain at t he time, winsts southwest a t lS knots, and
                temperature 19°C. '

                       Nova Scotia reported they obaerved .a very bright red baH, which was very high
                       in       aky and a bit larger than the ,sun, for a duration of approximately
                       fourtheseconds.

                                         Condition of Sky:                            Clear                             <t ~ft 1-- 3 ·. oo ,l;

                       their early thirties. A patrol was sept to the area but nothing unusual could
